I just hear it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that you're being punished for your father's decline is something that you might be able to change your mind about if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: No... I don't know. I just know what I hear. Like I said, when it gets quiet, I hear her out in the hallway. Just... \"You failed him.\" It's so clear. I've gotten up to check. No one's there, but... I don't know if she's wrong. I just hear her.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is a part of your own thoughts or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um... my sister wanted me to come in. She keeps showing me the doctor's notes and telling me I did everything right, but... I don't know. I just can't shake the certainty that I'm being punished. For letting my father decline. It just feels like... like some judgment has already been passed on me, no matter what she says.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your father's decline?\n\nPatient: I suppose so... I just... I keep replaying every decision about his appointments. And all those tense conversations with my relatives. It just... I don't know. I end up feeling like I'm just a selfish daughter. And a useless employee, really. I just... I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose so. Most days I just... I wake up with this heavy feeling. Like it's all hollow. The color is just... gone from everything. I sit at my desk at work, or... or I just stare out at the garden at home, and... it's like none of it even reaches me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don't really know... I guess I haven't paid much attention to that. I just... mostly I just go to work and come home. I don't really know how to explain it. Sometimes at night, when the house is completely quiet... I hear a woman. It sounds so clear, like she is standing right out in the hallway. But what she's saying... it feels real. I know I'm being punished for letting my father decline. I just hear it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that you're being punished for your father's decline is something that you might be able to change your mind about if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, my sister has already tried that. She showed me the doctor's notes. She tells me I did everything right. But... it doesn't change anything. I keep replaying every decision about his appointments. And all those tense conversations with my relatives. It just... I don't know. I end up feeling like I'm just a selfish daughter. And a useless employee, really. I just... I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose so. Most days I just... I wake up with this heavy feeling. Like it's all hollow. The color is just... gone from everything. I sit at my desk at work, or... or I just stare out at the garden at home, and... it's like none of it even reaches me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don't really know... I guess I haven't paid much attention to that. I just... mostly I just go to work and come home. So I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in things you are currently doing?\n\nPatient: Not really... I just do what needs to be done. I just get through the day, mostly. I don't really pay attention to whether I'm enjoying it or not.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know how to explain it. Sometimes at night, when the house is completely quiet... I hear a woman. It sounds so clear, like she is standing right out in the hallway. She tells me that I failed him. I have gotten up more than once to check, to see who is there, but... the hallway is always empty. I don't really like talking about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... a little over a year, I guess. Maybe a year and two months. It was... it started around the time my father got sick. His health and his behavior started changing, so... I had to step in to take care of him. I guess that's when it all started.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the woman's voice you hear at night is real?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know if she's a real person standing there. But what she's saying... it feels real. I know I'm being punished for letting my father decline. My sister tries to tell me I did everything right, she even shows me the doctor's notes, but... it doesn't change anything. I just know that some kind of judgment has already been passed on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that you're being punished for your father's decline is something that might not be true?\n\nPatient: No... I don't. Like I said, it doesn't matter what my sister says or what's written on a piece of paper. I just... I can't shake the certainty of it. I know I'm being punished. It's not just some idea that might be wrong, it's... it's already decided.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the voice you hear at night is just a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I hear it. When the house gets quiet at night, it's just... it's as clear as if someone is standing right out in the hallway. She just says, 'You failed him.' I've... I've actually gotten out of bed a few times to check. Even though nobody's there. So... I don't know where it comes from. I just hear it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that you're being punished for your father's decline is something that you might be able to change your mind about if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, my sister has already tried that. She showed me the doctor's notes. She tells me I did everything right. But... it doesn't change anything. I came in because I just can't shake the certainty of it. More information won't change the fact that... the judgment has already been passed on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear at night could ever be wrong when it says you failed your father?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I just know what I hear. Like I said, when it gets quiet, I hear her out in the hallway. Just... \"You failed him.\" It's so clear. I've gotten up to check. No one's there, but... I don't know if she's wrong. I just hear her.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is a part of your own thoughts or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. It doesn't really feel like my own thoughts. Like I was saying, it's a woman's voice. When it's quiet at night... it sounds like she's right there in the hallway. That's why I've gotten up more than once to look for her. Because it sounds completely separate from me. I wouldn't get out of bed to check on a thought.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for someone to sincerely believe they're being punished without it actually being true?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe for someone else. But for me... it's just... I already know it's true. Like I said, my sister tries to convince me otherwise. She shows me the notes and says I did fine, but... I just can't shake the certainty of it. The judgment has already been passed. It just... it feels too real to not be true.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your belief about being punished is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see how. I know my sister thinks it's not real. Like I just said, she keeps pulling out the doctor's notes, telling me I did everything right when my father was declining. But... I just can't shake the certainty of it. That I'm being punished. It just feels like... like the judgment has already been passed on me. I don't know how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is something that other people could hear if they were in the room with you?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I mean... I hear it. Like I said, when it's quiet at night... I hear her saying \"You failed him.\" It sounds like she's right there in the hallway. I don't know if anyone else would hear it... but it's clear enough that I get up to check. Even though no one is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear at night could be a part of your mind trying to process your guilt, rather than an actual external judgment?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. But... it doesn't feel like that. Like I said, when the house is quiet... it's just so clear. It's a woman's voice, and it sounds like she's physically out there in the hallway. That's why I keep getting up to check. I just... I don't think my mind could make it sound that real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your guilt about your father's decline is based on actual mistakes you made or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I just... I keep going over every decision I made about his appointments. Every tense conversation I had with my relatives about his care. I just... I feel like I'm a selfish daughter. And a useless employee at work, too. I just feel like I deserve whatever happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear and the idea that you're being punished could both be part of your own mind, rather than real external things?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I mean, maybe. I just... I never heard anything like this before. Or thought I was being punished. None of this started until... well, until after I got so down about my dad. It was months of just feeling... awful and guilty and tired before the voice started. So... maybe it is my mind. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe you are being punished for your father's decline?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I still believe it. I just... I can't shake the certainty that I'm being punished for letting him decline. My sister tries to talk me out of it... she shows me the doctor's notes, she tells me I did everything right. But... it doesn't change anything. It just... it feels like the judgment has already been passed on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
